2020-12-15 03:17:50  [TradingTheTrend: intro-to-options-trading #787347359385911296]  TradingTheTrend: TradingTheTrend — 12/15/20, 3:17 AM Tuesday, December 15, 2020 at 3:17 AM What is an option? An option is a contract giving the buyer the right to buy or sell an underlying asset at a specific price, on or before a certain date. An option is a security, and constitutes a binding contract with strictly defined terms and properties. Every option contract represents 100 shares. Options are time decaying securities. They are much riskier than trading stock, but offer much higher rewards. The bigger risk/reward is what draws in most traders/investors. Calls You buy calls when you're expecting a stock to be bullish. When you buy a call, you’re buying the right to purchase 100 shares of a particular stock at a predetermined price, which is the “strike price.” To purchase a call, you pay the seller a fee, known as a “premium. Puts You buy puts when you are expecting a stock to be bearish. A put option gives you the right, but not the obligation, to sell a stock at a certain price in the future (exercise). When you purchase a put, you expect the underlying asset to decline in price.. As price declines, your contracts will gain value. Puts lose value as the underlying increases. You are not obligated to "exercise" call or put options, and can simply sell them to gain the premium." (edited) Saturday, December 19, 2020 at 1:23 AM

2026-08-13 11:29:51  [ZTRADEZ (OPTIONS & STOCKS): 🔸︱🌗are-swings #1248264554886991893]  are alerts: are alerts APP — 11:29 AM Thursday, August 13, 2026 at 11:29 AM Remember how awful the market was 3-6 weeks ago? Getting chopped up, stopped out, setups having little to no follow through. There is absolutely nothing wrong with sizing down, or not trading at all when market is in a period like that. You want to save your capital and mental for when the market is trending, bulls are in control, and when it's time to put your foot on the gas. I am an analyst, so I am constantly looking for setups or rotations, but as an individual, I think you're better "sitting on your hands" when the market isn't favorable. We always say, patience pays. Remember this for the next time the market is tough, taking a month to size down or not trade isn't going to kill you. IF you plan on trading / being actively invested long term, you need to know when to participate in the market, and when to book a vacation. @unknown-role
